- **sahp-ch01-no-best-practices**: Frames architecture work as least-worst trade-off analysis rather than best-practice lookup, records decisions as lightweight ADRs, governs them with fitness functions (atomic, holistic, dynamic, manual), and treats data ownership as a first-class architecture concern. Based on Chapter 1 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when an architect asks for "the best" design, drafts an ADR, plans architecture governance or CI checks, separates operational from analytical data, or needs shared vocabulary before decomposing a monolith. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch01-no-best-practices))
- **sahp-ch02-discerning-coupling**: Untangles coupling before any distributed-architecture trade-off: the architecture quantum (independently deployable, functionally cohesive, statically coupled unit), static coupling via the bootstrap test, and dynamic coupling along communication, consistency and coordination with the eight named saga combinations. Based on Chapter 2 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when sizing services, counting real quanta, spotting shared database, mediator or UI coupling, choosing sync vs async calls, or framing a trade-off analysis. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch02-discerning-coupling))
- **sahp-ch03-architectural-modularity**: Guides when and why to break a system into smaller parts by tying decomposition to business drivers (speed-to-market, competitive advantage) through five characteristics: maintainability, testability, deployability, scalability/elasticity and fault tolerance. Based on Chapter 3 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when justifying or challenging a monolith migration, writing the business case or ADR for a split, choosing modular monolith vs. services, or checking whether inter-service coupling cancels the benefits. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch03-architectural-modularity))
- **sahp-ch04-architectural-decomposition**: Guides how to break a monolith apart: judging whether a codebase is decomposable with coupling metrics (afferent/efferent coupling, abstractness, instability, distance from the main sequence), then choosing between component-based decomposition and tactical forking, with service-based architecture as a stepping stone. Based on Chapter 4 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when planning a monolith migration, assessing a legacy codebase's structure, picking a decomposition approach, or writing the ADR for that choice. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch04-architectural-decomposition))
- **sahp-ch05-component-decomposition-patterns**: Applies six ordered refactoring patterns that turn a namespace-structured monolith into domain services (size components by statements, gather common domain logic, flatten namespaces, map component coupling, group into domains, extract domain services), each with CI fitness functions. Based on Chapter 5 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when sizing or cataloging components, judging migration feasibility and effort, deciding what to consolidate or flatten, grouping components into domains, or sequencing a move to service-based architecture. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch05-component-decomposition-patterns))
- **sahp-ch06-pulling-apart-operational-data**: Decides whether and how to split a monolithic operational database by weighing six data disintegrators against two integrators, then executes a five-step data-domain migration (schemas, connection isolation, separate servers) and picks a database type per domain. Based on Chapter 6 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when justifying a database split, mapping tables to data domains, finding cross-domain foreign keys or views, sizing connection quotas, planning schema migration, or choosing relational versus NoSQL, NewSQL, cloud or time-series… ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch06-pulling-apart-operational-data))
- **sahp-ch07-service-granularity**: Decides how big services should be by weighing six granularity disintegrators (scope, volatility, scalability, fault tolerance, security, extensibility) against four integrators (ACID transactions, workflow, shared domain code, data relationships), then framing the conflict as business trade-off statements. Based on Chapter 7 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when splitting or merging services, challenging "make it smaller" or "single responsibility" arguments, sizing microservices, or writing a granularity ADR. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch07-service-granularity))
- **sahp-ch08-reuse-patterns**: Guidance for sharing code and functionality across distributed services, based on Chapter 8 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Covers code replication, shared libraries (granularity, versioning, deprecation), shared services (runtime change risk, latency, scaling, availability), sidecars and service mesh for operational coupling, and when reuse adds value (abstraction versus rate of change, platforms). Use when deciding where common code should live, splitting a shared library, choosing library versus service, or reviewing a sidecar or reuse pr… ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch08-reuse-patterns))
- **sahp-ch09-data-ownership-and-transactions**: Assigns table ownership after a database split (single, common and joint ownership; table split, shared data domain, delegate and service consolidation) and manages distributed transactions with ACID vs BASE reasoning and three eventual consistency patterns (background sync, orchestrated request-based, event-based). Based on Chapter 9 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when deciding which service owns a table, resolving several services writing one table, or choosing how to keep service-owned data consistent. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch09-data-ownership-and-transactions))
- **sahp-ch10-distributed-data-access**: Guides how a service reads data owned by another service once databases are split, comparing four read-access patterns (inter-service communication, column schema replication, replicated in-memory caching, shared data domain) on latency, coupling, consistency, ownership, volume and security. Based on Chapter 10 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when a service needs data it does not own, when a cross-service join disappears after a data split, when sizing a replicated cache, or when writing an ADR for read access. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch10-distributed-data-access))
- **sahp-ch11-distributed-workflows**: Guidance for choosing and designing coordination of multi-service workflows (orchestration vs choreography), based on Chapter 11 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Covers error-path analysis, semantic vs implementation coupling, workflow state options in choreography (front controller, stateless, stamp coupling) and scenario-driven trade-off tables. Use when deciding who coordinates a distributed workflow, modeling its failure paths, placing workflow state, or writing the ADR for that choice. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch11-distributed-workflows))
- **sahp-ch12-transactional-sagas**: Guides the choice and implementation of distributed transactional sagas using the eight combinations of communication (sync/async), consistency (atomic/eventual) and coordination (orchestrated/choreographed), compensating updates versus state management, saga state machines and saga tagging. Based on Chapter 12 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when designing a multi-service workflow, picking a saga pattern, replacing a slow atomic saga, handling failed steps, or auditing which services join which saga. ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch12-transactional-sagas))
- **sahp-ch13-contracts**: Guides choosing and governing integration contracts in distributed systems: placing each contract on the strict-to-loose spectrum, versioning, consumer-driven contracts as fitness functions, stamp coupling (over-coupling, bandwidth cost, and its legitimate use for choreographed workflow state). Based on Chapter 13 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when designing or reviewing APIs, message schemas or event payloads between services, deciding gRPC vs REST vs JSON maps, auditing payload bloat, or carrying state through a choreographed workflo… ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch13-contracts))
- **sahp-ch14-analytical-data**: Guidance for serving analytical data (BI, ML training) from distributed architectures: why centralized Data Warehouse and Data Lake designs fail with domain-partitioned services, the four Data Mesh principles, data product quantum (DPQ) types, cooperative-quantum coupling rules, and completeness fitness functions. Based on Chapter 14 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Use when designing analytics for microservices, choosing warehouse vs lake vs mesh, designing a DPQ or its contracts, or reviewing analytical pipelines for brittleness, PII or sta… ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch14-analytical-data))
- **sahp-ch15-trade-off-analysis**: Gives a repeatable method for building custom trade-off analyses in distributed architectures, based on Chapter 15 of Ford, Richards, Sadalage and Dehghani's "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts". Covers finding entangled dimensions, static coupling inventories, rating options in isolation then in a matrix, MECE comparisons, the out-of-context trap, domain scenario modeling, bottom-line framing for stakeholders and resisting evangelism. Use when comparing architecture options, building a trade-off table, presenting a decision to non-technical stakeholders, or challenging a silver-bullet pro… ([sauloleite/booksasskills](https://github.com/sauloleite/booksasskills/tree/HEAD/software-architecture-the-hard-parts-ford-et-al/sahp-ch15-trade-off-analysis))
- **sahp-complete**: Whole-book guide to "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts" (Ford, Richards, Sadalage, Dehghani) for distributed architecture decisions without best practices. Routes to per-topic references from all 15 chapters and bundles 13 tested scripts. - **sahp-theme-data-decomposition-and-analytics**: Theme skill on splitting operational data and designing analytical data from "Software Architecture: The Hard Parts" (Ford, Richards, Sadalage, Dehghani) (chapters 6 and 14).
